\displaystyle\bm{\beta}_{\perp}[\mathbf{0}_{d-r_{0}\times r_{0}},\mathbf{I}_{d-r_{0}}][-\bm{\alpha}(\tau),\bm{\Gamma}_{\tau}(1)\bm{\beta}_{\perp}]^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bm{\beta}_{\perp}[\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\bm{\Gamma}_{\tau}(1)\bm{\beta}_{\perp}]^{-1}\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau),

where the last step follows from

\displaystyle[-\bm{\alpha}(\tau),\bm{\Gamma}_{\tau}(1)\bm{\beta}_{\perp}]^{-1}=\left[\begin{matrix}(\bm{\alpha}^{\top}(\tau)\bm{\alpha}(\tau))^{-1}\bm{\alpha}^{\top}(\tau)\left\{\bm{\Gamma}_{\tau}(1)\bm{\beta}_{\perp}[\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\bm{\Gamma}_{\tau}(1)\bm{\beta}_{\perp}]^{-1}\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau)-\mathbf{I}_{d}\right\}\\
[\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\bm{\Gamma}_{\tau}(1)\bm{\beta}_{\perp}]^{-1}\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\end{matrix}\right].

Recall that
\mathbf{I}_{d}=\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}+\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}
, so
\Delta\mathbf{y}_{t}=\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\Delta\mathbf{y}_{t}+\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\Delta\mathbf{y}_{t}
. In connection with
\mathbf{z}_{t}=\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\mathbf{y}_{t}+\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\Delta\mathbf{y}_{t}
, we have
\Delta\mathbf{y}_{t}=\mathbf{z}_{t}-\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\mathbf{z}_{t-1}
,
\overline{\bm{\beta}}_{\perp}^{\top}\Delta\mathbf{y}_{t}=\overline{\bm{\beta}}_{\perp}^{\top}\mathbf{z}_{t}
and
\Delta\mathbf{y}_{t}=\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\mathbf{z}_{t}+\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\Delta\mathbf{y}_{t}
. Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbf{y}_{t}=\mathbf{y}_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{t}\Delta\mathbf{y}_{i}=\mathbf{y}_{0}+\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\sum_{j=1}^{t}\mathbf{z}_{j}+\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\mathbf{y}_{t}-\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\mathbf{y}_{0}=\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\sum_{j=1}^{t}\mathbf{z}_{j}+\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\mathbf{z}_{t}+\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\mathbf{y}_{0}.

The proof of parts (1)-(2) is now completed.

(3). Define
\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t}(\tau):=\mathbf{J}^{-1}\mathbf{P}(L)\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{t}(\tau)=\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{t}(\tau)+\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\Delta\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{t}(\tau)
. Write

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}_{\tau}(L)\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{t}(\tau)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}(\tau)+\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{t}(\tau),

\displaystyle\mathbf{J}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{\tau}^{*}(L)\mathbf{P}_{\tau}(L)\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{t}(\tau)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{t}(\tau),

\displaystyle\mathbf{J}^{-1}\mathbf{B}_{\tau}^{*}(L)\mathbf{J}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t}(\tau)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{t}(\tau),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t}(\tau)=\mathbf{B}_{\tau}^{-1}(L)\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{t}(\tau)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\bm{\Psi}_{j}(\tau)\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{t-j}(\tau).

Hence,
\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t}(\tau)
is a stationary VAR
(p)
process which has an MA
(\infty)
representation. Similar to the proof of part (1) and additionally by BN decomposition, we have

\displaystyle\Delta\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{t}(\tau)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\Delta\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{t}(\tau)+\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\Delta\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{t}(\tau)=\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t}(\tau)+\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\Delta\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{t}(\tau)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}(\bm{\Psi}_{\tau}(1)\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{t}(\tau)-\bm{\Psi}_{\tau}^{*}(L)\Delta\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{t}(\tau))+\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\Delta\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{t}(\tau),

where
\bm{\Psi}_{\tau}(L):=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\bm{\Psi}_{j}(\tau)L^{j}=\mathbf{B}_{\tau}^{-1}(L)
,
\bm{\Psi}_{\tau}^{*}(L)=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\bm{\Psi}_{j}^{*}(\tau)L^{j}
and
\bm{\Psi}_{j}^{*}(\tau)=\sum_{i=j+1}^{\infty}\bm{\Psi}_{i}(\tau)
.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{t}(\tau)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\left[\bm{\Psi}_{\tau}(1)\sum_{i=1}^{t}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}(\tau)-\bm{\Psi}_{\tau}^{*}(L)\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{t}(\tau)+\bm{\Psi}_{\tau}^{*}(L)\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{0}(\tau)\right]+\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t}(\tau)+\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{0}(\tau)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\bm{\Psi}_{\tau}(1)\sum_{i=1}^{t}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}(\tau)+\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\bm{\Psi}_{\tau}(1)\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{t}(\tau)-\bm{\Psi}_{\tau}^{*}(L)\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{t}+\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\bm{\Psi}_{\tau}^{*}(L)\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{t-1}(\tau)+\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{0}^{*}(\tau),

where
\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{0}^{*}(\tau)=\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\bm{\Psi}_{\tau}^{*}(L)\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{0}(\tau)+\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{0}(\tau)
.

By the proof of (1)-(2), we have
\Delta\mathbf{y}_{t}=\mathbf{z}_{t}-\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\mathbf{z}_{t-1}
and
\mathbf{z}_{t}=\sum_{i=1}^{p}\mathbf{B}_{i}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{z}_{t-i}+\mathbf{u}_{t}
. Let
\mathbf{B}(\tau)
be the companion matrix of
\mathbf{z}_{t}
such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{B}(\tau)=\left[\begin{matrix}\mathbf{B}_{1}(\tau)&\cdots&\mathbf{B}_{p_{0}-1}(\tau)&\mathbf{B}_{p_{0}}(\tau)\\
\mathbf{I}_{d}&\cdots&\mathbf{0}_{d}&\mathbf{0}_{d}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
\mathbf{0}_{d}&\cdots&\mathbf{I}_{d}&\mathbf{0}_{d}\\
\end{matrix}\right]

and thus
\mathbf{z}_{t}=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\overline{\mathbf{J}}\prod_{i=0}^{j-1}\mathbf{B}(\tau_{t-m})\overline{\mathbf{J}}^{\top}\mathbf{v}(\tau_{t-j})+\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\overline{\mathbf{J}}\prod_{i=0}^{j-1}\mathbf{B}(\tau_{t-m})\overline{\mathbf{J}}^{\top}\bm{\omega}(\tau_{t-j})\bm{\varepsilon}_{t-j}
, where
\overline{\mathbf{J}}=[\mathbf{I}_{d},\mathbf{0}_{d\times d(p_{0}-1)}]
.

Define
\rho_{B}=\max\{|\rho|:\rho\ \text{is the eigenvalue of}\ \mathbf{B}(\tau)\}
. Since
\mathrm{det}(\mathbf{B}_{\tau}(L))\neq 0
for all
|L|\leq 1
, we have
\rho_{B}<1
. Similar to the proof of Proposition 2.4 in
Dahlhaus & Polonik 2009
, we have
\max_{t\geq 1}|\prod_{m=0}^{j-1}\mathbf{B}(\tau_{t-m})|\leq M\rho_{B}^{j}
. Note that
\Delta\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{t}(\tau)=\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t}(\tau)-\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t-1}(\tau)
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t}(\tau)=\sum_{i=1}^{p}\mathbf{B}_{i}(\tau)\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t-i}(\tau)+\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{t}(\tau)=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\overline{\mathbf{J}}\mathbf{B}^{j}(\tau)\overline{\mathbf{J}}^{\top}\bm{\omega}(\tau)\bm{\varepsilon}_{t-j}
.
For any conformable matrices
\{\mathbf{A}_{i}\}
and
\{\mathbf{B}_{i}\}
, since

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{r}\mathbf{A}_{i}-\prod_{i=1}^{r}\mathbf{B}_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{r}\left(\prod_{k=1}^{j-1}\mathbf{A}_{k}\right)\left(\mathbf{A}_{j}-\mathbf{B}_{j}\right)\left(\prod_{k=j+1}^{r}\mathbf{B}_{k}\right),

we then obtain

\displaystyle\left|\overline{\mathbf{J}}\prod_{i=0}^{j-1}\mathbf{B}(\tau_{t-i})\overline{\mathbf{J}}^{\top}\bm{\omega}(\tau_{t-j})-\overline{\mathbf{J}}\mathbf{B}^{j}(\tau_{t})\overline{\mathbf{J}}^{\top}\bm{\omega}(\tau_{t})\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\left(\overline{\mathbf{J}}\prod_{i=0}^{j-1}\mathbf{B}(\tau_{t-i})\overline{\mathbf{J}}^{\top}-\overline{\mathbf{J}}\mathbf{B}^{j}(\tau_{t})\overline{\mathbf{J}}^{\top}\right)\bm{\omega}(\tau_{t})+\overline{\mathbf{J}}\prod_{i=0}^{j-1}\mathbf{B}(\tau_{t-i})\overline{\mathbf{J}}^{\top}\left(\bm{\omega}(\tau_{t-j})-\bm{\omega}(\tau_{t})\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M\sum_{i=1}^{j-1}\left|\mathbf{B}^{i}(\tau_{t})(\mathbf{B}(\tau_{t-i})-\mathbf{B}(\tau_{t}))\prod_{k=i+1}^{j-1}\mathbf{B}(\tau_{t-k})\right|+M\rho_{B}^{j}\frac{j}{T}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M\sum_{i=1}^{j-1}\frac{i}{T}\rho_{B}^{j-1}+M\rho_{B}^{j}\frac{j}{T}.

In addition, by Minkowski inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{z}_{t}-\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t}(\tau_{t})\right\|_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\left|\overline{\mathbf{J}}\prod_{i=0}^{j-1}\mathbf{B}(\tau_{t-i})\overline{\mathbf{J}}^{\top}\bm{\omega}(\tau_{t-j})-\overline{\mathbf{J}}\mathbf{B}^{j}(\tau_{t})\overline{\mathbf{J}}^{\top}\bm{\omega}(\tau_{t})\right|\cdot\left\|\bm{\varepsilon}_{t}\right\|_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{j-1}\frac{i}{T}\rho_{B}^{j-1}+\rho_{B}^{j}\frac{j}{T}\right)=O\left(T^{-1}\right).

Similarly, we have
\|\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t}(\tau)-\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t}(\tau^{\prime})\|_{\delta}=O(|\tau-\tau^{\prime}|)
.

By the proof of (1)-(2), we have
\Delta\mathbf{y}_{t}=\mathbf{z}_{t}-\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\mathbf{z}_{t-1}
and
\Delta\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{t}(\tau)=\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t}(\tau)-\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t-1}(\tau)
. Then, since
\max_{t}\|\mathbf{z}_{t}-\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t}(\tau_{t})\|_{\delta}=O(1/T)
and
\|\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t}(\tau)-\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t}(\tau^{\prime})\|_{\delta}=O(|\tau-\tau^{\prime}|)
for any
\tau,\tau^{\prime}\in[0,1]
, we have
\max_{t}\|\Delta\mathbf{y}_{t}-\Delta\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{t}(\tau_{t})\|_{\delta}=O(1/T)
and
\|\Delta\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{t}(\tau)-\Delta\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{t}(\tau^{\prime})\|_{\delta}=O(|\tau-\tau^{\prime}|)
.

Since
\Delta\mathbf{y}_{t}=\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\mathbf{z}_{t}+\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\Delta\mathbf{y}_{t}
and
\bm{\beta}^{\top}\mathbf{y}_{t}=\bm{\beta}^{\top}\mathbf{z}_{t}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{y}_{t}=\mathbf{y}_{0}+\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\sum_{j=1}^{t}\mathbf{z}_{j}+\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\mathbf{z}_{t}-\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\mathbf{z}_{0}

and

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{t}\Delta\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{j}(\tau_{j})=\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\sum_{j=1}^{t}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{j}(\tau_{j})+\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t}(\tau_{t})-\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{0}(\tau_{1})+\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}(\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{j}(\tau_{j})-\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{j}(\tau_{j+1})).

Hence, by part (1) we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{y}_{t}-\mathbf{y}_{0}-\sum_{j=1}^{t}\Delta\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{t}(\tau_{j})\|_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\sum_{j=1}^{t}(\mathbf{z}_{j}-\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{j}(\tau_{j}))\|_{\delta}+\|\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\sum_{j=1}^{t-1}(\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{j}(\tau_{j})-\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{j}(\tau_{j+1}))\|_{\delta}+O(1/T)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{T,1}+I_{T,2}+O(1/T).

For
I_{T,1}
, as
\{(\mathbf{J}\prod_{i=0}^{j-1}\mathbf{B}(\tau_{t-i})\mathbf{J}^{\top}\bm{\omega}(\tau_{t-j})-\mathbf{J}\mathbf{B}^{j}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{J}^{\top}\bm{\omega}(\tau_{t}))\bm{\varepsilon}_{t-j}\}_{t}
is a sequence of martingale differences, by Burkholder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left|\sum_{j=1}^{t}(\mathbf{z}_{j}-\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{j}(\tau_{j}))\right|\right|_{\delta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\left|\sum_{j=1}^{t}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(\mathbf{J}\prod_{i=0}^{k-1}\mathbf{B}(\tau_{j-i})\mathbf{J}^{\top}\bm{\omega}(\tau_{j-k})-\mathbf{J}\mathbf{B}^{k}(\tau_{j})\mathbf{J}^{\top}\bm{\omega}(\tau_{j}))\bm{\varepsilon}_{j-k}\right|\right|_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left|\left|\sum_{j=1}^{t}(\mathbf{J}\prod_{i=0}^{k-1}\mathbf{B}(\tau_{j-i})\mathbf{J}^{\top}\bm{\omega}(\tau_{j-k})-\mathbf{J}\mathbf{B}^{k}(\tau_{j})\mathbf{J}^{\top}\bm{\omega}(\tau_{j}))\bm{\varepsilon}_{j-k}\right|\right|_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}O(\sqrt{T})\max_{j}\left|\mathbf{J}\prod_{i=0}^{k-1}\mathbf{B}(\tau_{j-i})\mathbf{J}^{\top}\bm{\omega}(\tau_{j-k})-\mathbf{J}\mathbf{B}^{k}(\tau_{j})\mathbf{J}^{\top}\bm{\omega}(\tau_{j})\right|=O(1/\sqrt{T}).

Hence,
I_{T,1}=O(1/\sqrt{T})
. Similarly, we have
I_{T,2}=O(1/\sqrt{T})
. The proof of part (3) is now completed.

(4). By (1)-(3) of this lemma, we have
\sup_{u\in[0,1]}|\mathbf{z}_{\lfloor Tu\rfloor}|\leq\left\{\sum_{t=1}^{T}|\mathbf{z}_{t}|^{\delta}\right\}^{1/\delta}=O_{P}(T^{1/\delta})
and

\displaystyle\sup_{u\in[0,1]}\left|\sum_{t=1}^{\lfloor Tu\rfloor}(\mathbf{z}_{t}-\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t}(\tau_{t}))|\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}|\mathbf{z}_{t}-\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t}(\tau_{t})\right|=O_{P}(1).

Then, we have uniformly over
u\in[0,1]

\displaystyle T^{-1/2}\mathbf{y}_{\lfloor Tu\rfloor}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}T^{-1/2}\sum_{t=1}^{\lfloor Tu\rfloor}\mathbf{z}_{t}+T^{-1/2}\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\mathbf{z}_{\lfloor Tu\rfloor}+T^{-1/2}\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\mathbf{y}_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}T^{-1/2}\sum_{t=1}^{\lfloor Tu\rfloor}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\bm{\Psi}_{j}(\tau_{t})\bm{\omega}(\tau_{t})\bm{\varepsilon}_{t-j}+O_{P}(T^{1/\delta-1/2})

with
|\bm{\Psi}_{j}(\tau)|
converging to zero exponentially. By BN decomposition, we have
\bm{\Psi}_{\tau}(L):=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\bm{\Psi}_{j}(\tau)L^{j}=\bm{\Psi}_{\tau}(1)-(1-L)\bm{\Psi}_{\tau}^{*}(L)
with
\bm{\Psi}_{\tau}^{*}(L)=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\bm{\Psi}_{j}^{*}(\tau)L^{j}
and
\bm{\Psi}_{j}^{*}(\tau)=\sum_{i=j+1}^{\infty}\bm{\Psi}_{i}(\tau)
. Hence,

\displaystyle T^{-1/2}\sum_{t=1}^{\lfloor Tu\rfloor}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\bm{\Psi}_{j}(\tau_{t})\bm{\omega}(\tau_{t})\bm{\varepsilon}_{t-j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T^{-1/2}\sum_{t=1}^{\lfloor Tu\rfloor}\bm{\Psi}_{\tau_{t}}(1)\bm{\omega}(\tau_{t})\bm{\varepsilon}_{t}+T^{-1/2}\bm{\Psi}_{\tau_{1}}^{*}(L)\bm{\omega}(\tau_{1})\bm{\varepsilon}_{0}-T^{-1/2}\bm{\Psi}_{\tau_{\lfloor Tu\rfloor}}^{*}(L)\bm{\omega}(\tau_{\lfloor Tu\rfloor})\bm{\varepsilon}_{\lfloor Tu\rfloor}

\displaystyle+T^{-1/2}\sum_{t=1}^{\lfloor Tu\rfloor-1}\left(\bm{\Psi}_{\tau_{t+1}}(L)\bm{\omega}(\tau_{t+1})-\bm{\Psi}_{\tau_{t}}(L)\bm{\omega}(\tau_{t})\right)\bm{\varepsilon}_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{T,3}+\mathbf{I}_{T,4}+\mathbf{I}_{T,5}+\mathbf{I}_{T,6}.

By the usual functional central limit theory for martingale difference sequences (e.g., Theorem 4.1 in
Hall & Heyde 1980
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}_{T,3}\Rightarrow\int_{0}^{u}\bm{\Psi}_{s}(1)\bm{\omega}(s)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{W}_{d}(s).

Note that the Lindeberg condition can be verified as
\|\bm{\Psi}_{\tau_{t}}(1)\bm{\omega}(\tau_{t})\bm{\varepsilon}_{t}\|_{\delta}<\infty
for some
\delta>2
, and the convergence of conditional variance can be verified as
E(\bm{\varepsilon}_{t}\bm{\varepsilon}_{t}^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1})=\mathbf{I}_{d}
a.s.

In addition, we have
\mathbf{I}_{T,4}=O_{P}(T^{-1/2})
uniformly over
u\in[0,1]
as

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\Psi}_{\tau_{1}}^{*}(L)\bm{\omega}(\tau_{1})\bm{\varepsilon}_{0}\right\|_{\delta}\leq O(1)\sup_{\tau\in[0,1]}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}j|\bm{\Psi}_{j}(\tau)|<\infty.

For
\mathbf{I}_{T,5}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{u\in[0,1]}\left|T^{-1/2}\bm{\Psi}_{\tau_{\lfloor Tu\rfloor}}^{*}(L)\bm{\omega}(\tau_{\lfloor Tu\rfloor})\bm{\varepsilon}_{\lfloor Tu\rfloor}\right|\leq T^{-1/2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}|\bm{\Psi}_{\tau_{t}}^{*}(L)\bm{\omega}(\tau_{t})\bm{\varepsilon}_{t}|=O_{P}(T^{1/\delta-1/2}).

Finally, for
\mathbf{I}_{T,6}
, as
\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\left\|\left(\bm{\Psi}_{\tau_{t+1}}(L)\bm{\omega}(\tau_{t+1})-\bm{\Psi}_{\tau_{t}}(L)\bm{\omega}(\tau_{t})\right)\bm{\varepsilon}_{t}\right\|_{\delta}<\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{u\in[0,1]}\|\mathbf{I}_{T,6}\|\leq\sup_{u\in[0,1]}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{\lfloor Tu\rfloor-1}\left\|\left(\widetilde{\mathbb{B}}_{t+1}(L)-\widetilde{\mathbb{B}}_{t}(L)\right)\bm{\epsilon}_{t}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\left\|\left(\bm{\Psi}_{\tau_{t+1}}(L)\bm{\omega}(\tau_{t+1})-\bm{\Psi}_{\tau_{t}}(L)\bm{\omega}(\tau_{t})\right)\bm{\varepsilon}_{t}\right\|=O(1/\sqrt{T}).

The proof of part (4) is now completed.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.1
.

(1). Recall we have defined many relevant notations in Appendix
A.2
. Then it is easy to know that

\mathbf{Q}^{-1}(\tau)=\left[\bm{\beta}_{\perp}(\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\bm{\beta}_{\perp})^{-1},\bm{\alpha}(\tau)(\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\alpha}(\tau))^{-1}\right].

Let

\mathbf{M}(\tau_{t})=[\bm{\Pi}(\tau_{t}),\bm{\Gamma}(\tau_{t})]-[\bm{\Pi}(\tau),\bm{\Gamma}(\tau)]-[\bm{\Pi}^{(1)}(\tau),\bm{\Gamma}^{(1)}(\tau)](\tau_{t}-\tau)-\frac{1}{2}[\bm{\Pi}^{(2)}(\tau),\bm{\Gamma}^{(2)}(\tau)](\tau_{t}-\tau)^{2}.

Also, define
\mathbf{h}_{t}^{*}=\mathbf{h}_{t}\otimes[1,\frac{\tau_{t+1}-\tau}{h}]^{\top}
,
\mathbf{V}_{T}(\tau)=\left[\mathbf{V}_{T,0}(\tau),\mathbf{V}_{T,1}(\tau)\right]
and

\mathbf{S}_{T}(\tau)=\left[\begin{matrix}\mathbf{S}_{T,0}(\tau)&\mathbf{S}_{T,1}(\tau)\\
\mathbf{S}_{T,1}(\tau)&\mathbf{S}_{T,2}(\tau)\end{matrix}\right].

We now proceed, and write

\displaystyle[\widehat{\bm{\Pi}}(\tau),\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}(\tau)]\mathbf{Q}^{*,-1}(\tau)\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*}(\tau)\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}_{T}(\tau)\left(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{Q}^{*,\top}(\tau)\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*}(\tau)\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*,-1}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*}\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*,\top}(\tau)\mathbf{Q}^{*,-1,\top}(\tau)\right)\mathbf{S}_{T}^{+}(\tau)\left(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{Q}^{*,-1}(\tau)\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*}(\tau)\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*}\right)\times[\mathbf{I}_{dp_{0}},\mathbf{0}_{dp_{0}}]^{\top}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[\bm{\Pi}(\tau),\bm{\Gamma}(\tau),h\bm{\Pi}^{(1)}(\tau),h\bm{\Gamma}^{(1)}(\tau)]\left(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{Q}^{*,-1}(\tau)\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*}(\tau)\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*,-1}\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*,\top}(\tau)\mathbf{Q}^{*}(\tau)\right)\mathbf{S}_{T}(\tau)\left(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{Q}^{*,\top}(\tau)\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*}(\tau)\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*,-1}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*}\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*,\top}(\tau)\mathbf{Q}^{*,-1,\top}(\tau)\right)\mathbf{S}_{T}^{+}(\tau)\left(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{Q}^{*,-1}(\tau)\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*}(\tau)\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*}\right)[\mathbf{I}_{dp_{0}},\mathbf{0}_{dp_{0}}]^{\top}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}h^{2}[\bm{\Pi}^{(2)}(\tau),\bm{\Gamma}^{(2)}(\tau)]\mathbf{Q}^{*,-1}(\tau)\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*}(\tau)\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*}

\displaystyle\times\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*,-1}\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*,\top}(\tau)\mathbf{Q}^{*}(\tau)[\mathbf{S}_{T,2}(\tau),\mathbf{S}_{T,3}(\tau)]\left(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{Q}^{*,\top}(\tau)\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*}(\tau)\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*,-1}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*}\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*,\top}(\tau)\mathbf{Q}^{*,-1,\top}(\tau)\right)\mathbf{S}_{T}^{+}(\tau)\left(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{Q}^{*,-1}(\tau)\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*}(\tau)\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*}\right)[\mathbf{I}_{dp_{0}},\mathbf{0}_{dp_{0}}]^{\top}

\displaystyle+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{M}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{h}_{t-1}\mathbf{h}_{t-1}^{*,\top}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)\left(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{Q}^{*,\top}(\tau)\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*}(\tau)\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*,-1}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*}\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*,\top}(\tau)\mathbf{Q}^{*,-1,\top}(\tau)\right)\mathbf{S}_{T}^{+}(\tau)\left(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{Q}^{*,-1}(\tau)\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*}(\tau)\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*}\right)[\mathbf{I}_{dp_{0}},\mathbf{0}_{dp_{0}}]^{\top}

\displaystyle+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{u}_{t}\mathbf{h}_{t-1}^{*,\top}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)\left(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{Q}^{*,\top}(\tau)\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*}(\tau)\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*,-1}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*}\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*,\top}(\tau)\mathbf{Q}^{*,-1,\top}(\tau)\right)\mathbf{S}_{T}^{+}(\tau)\left(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{Q}^{*,-1}(\tau)\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*}(\tau)\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*}\right)[\mathbf{I}_{dp_{0}},\mathbf{0}_{dp_{0}}]^{\top}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{J}_{T,1}+\mathbf{J}_{T,2}+\mathbf{J}_{T,3}+\mathbf{J}_{T,4}.

Consider
\mathbf{J}_{T,1}
. By Lemma
A.1
,

\left(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*,-1}\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*,\top}(\tau)\mathbf{Q}^{*}(\tau)\right)\mathbf{S}_{T}(\tau)\left(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{Q}^{*,\top}(\tau)\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*}(\tau)\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*,-1}\right)
is asymptotically non-singular and thus

\mathbf{J}_{T,1}=[\bm{\Pi}(\tau),\bm{\Gamma}(\tau)]\mathbf{Q}^{*,-1}(\tau)\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*}(\tau)\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*}.

Consider
\mathbf{J}_{T,2}
. Note that
\bm{\Pi}^{(j)}(\tau)=\bm{\alpha}^{(j)}(\tau)\bm{\beta}^{\top}
and thus

[\bm{\Pi}^{(j)}(\tau),\bm{\Gamma}^{(j)}(\tau)]\mathbf{Q}^{*,-1}(\tau)=[\bm{\alpha}^{(j)}(\tau)\bm{\beta}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}^{-1}(\tau),\bm{\Gamma}^{(j)}(\tau)]=[\mathbf{0}_{d\times(d-r_{0})},\bm{\alpha}^{(j)}(\tau),\bm{\Gamma}^{(j)}(\tau)].

By Lemma
A.1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*,-1}\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*,\top}(\tau)\mathbf{Q}^{*}(\tau)[\mathbf{S}_{T,2}(\tau),\mathbf{S}_{T,3}(\tau)]\left(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{Q}^{*,\top}(\tau)\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*}(\tau)\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*,-1}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*}\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*,\top}(\tau)\mathbf{Q}^{*,-1,\top}(\tau)\right)\mathbf{S}_{T}^{+}(\tau)\left(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{Q}^{*,-1}(\tau)\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*}(\tau)\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*}\right)[\mathbf{I}_{dp_{0}+1},\mathbf{0}_{dp_{0}+1}]^{\top}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\begin{matrix}O_{P}(1)&\mathbf{0}_{(d-r_{0})\times(r_{0}+d(p_{0}-1))}+O_{P}(1/\sqrt{Th})&\\
\mathbf{0}_{(r_{0}+d(p_{0}-1))\times(d-r_{0})}+O_{P}(1/\sqrt{Th})&\widetilde{c}_{2}\mathbf{I}_{(r_{0}+d(p_{0}-1))}+O_{P}(h^{2}+1/\sqrt{Th})\\
\end{matrix}\right]

and thus

\displaystyle\mathbf{J}_{T,2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}h^{2}[\mathbf{0}_{d\times(d-r_{0})},\sqrt{Th}\bm{\alpha}^{(2)}(\tau),\sqrt{Th}\bm{\Gamma}^{(2)}(\tau)]

\displaystyle\times\left[\begin{matrix}O_{P}(1)&\mathbf{0}_{(d-r_{0})\times(r_{0}+d(p_{0}-1))}+O_{P}(1/\sqrt{Th})&\\
\mathbf{0}_{(r_{0}+d(p_{0}-1))\times(d-r_{0})}+O_{P}(1/\sqrt{Th})&\widetilde{c}_{2}\mathbf{I}_{(r_{0}+d(p_{0}-1))}+O_{P}(h^{2}+1/\sqrt{Th})\\
\end{matrix}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{Th}h^{2}\widetilde{c}_{2}[\mathbf{0}_{d\times(d-r_{0})},\bm{\alpha}^{(2)}(\tau),\bm{\Gamma}^{(2)}(\tau)]+O_{P}(h^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{Th}h^{2}\widetilde{c}_{2}[\bm{\alpha}^{(2)}(\tau)\bm{\beta}^{\top},\bm{\Gamma}^{(2)}(\tau)]\mathbf{Q}^{*,-1}(\tau)+O_{P}(h^{2}).

Consider
\mathbf{J}_{T,3}
. Note that
\bm{\Pi}^{(j)}(\tau)=\bm{\alpha}^{(j)}(\tau)\bm{\beta}^{\top}
and thus
\bm{\Pi}^{(j)}(\tau)\mathbf{Q}^{-1}(\tau)=[\mathbf{0}_{d\times(d-r_{0})},\bm{\mathbf{\alpha}}^{(j)}(\tau)]
. Let
\mathbf{M}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{Q}^{*,-1}(\tau)=[\mathbf{0}_{d\times(d-r_{0})},\mathbf{M}_{1}(\tau_{t}),\mathbf{M}_{2}(\tau_{t})]
such that
\mathbf{M}_{1}(\tau_{t})
is
d\times r_{0}
and
\mathbf{M}_{2}(\tau_{t})
is
d\times d(p_{0}-1)
. Hence, by using similar arguments as in the proof of Lemma
A.1
,
\mathbf{M}_{1}(\tau_{t})=O(h^{3})
and
\mathbf{M}_{2}(\tau_{t})=O(h^{3})
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{J}_{T,3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{M}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{Q}^{*,-1}(\tau)\mathbf{Q}^{*}(\tau)\mathbf{h}_{t-1}\mathbf{h}_{t-1}^{*,\top}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)\left(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{Q}^{*,\top}(\tau)\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*}(\tau)\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*,-1}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*}\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*,\top}(\tau)\mathbf{Q}^{*,-1,\top}(\tau)\right)\mathbf{S}_{T}^{+}(\tau)\left(\mathbf{I}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{Q}^{*,-1}(\tau)\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*}(\tau)\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*}\right)[\mathbf{I}_{dp_{0}},\mathbf{0}_{dp_{0}}]^{\top}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\mathbf{M}_{1}(\tau_{t})\bm{\beta}^{\top}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}+\mathbf{M}_{2}(\tau_{t})\Delta\mathbf{x}_{t-1}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left[\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top}\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}(\tau)\bm{\Xi}_{T}(\tau)\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1},\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\mathbf{w}_{t-1}\right]\otimes\left[1,\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right]K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)\cdot O_{P}(1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[O_{P}(h^{3}),O_{P}(h^{3}\sqrt{Th})].

Consider
\mathbf{J}_{T,4}
. Write

\displaystyle\left[\begin{matrix}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{u}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)&\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{u}_{t}\Delta\mathbf{x}_{t-1}^{\top}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)\end{matrix}\right]\mathbf{Q}^{*,\top}(\tau)\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*}(\tau)\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*,-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\begin{matrix}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{u}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top}\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}(\tau)\bm{\Xi}_{T}(\tau)\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)&\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{u}_{t}\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{\top}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)\end{matrix}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[\mathbf{J}_{T,41},\mathbf{J}_{T,42}],

where
\mathbf{w}_{t}=[\mathbf{z}_{t}^{\top}\bm{\beta},\Delta\mathbf{x}_{t}^{\top}]^{\top}
. For
\mathbf{J}_{T,42}
, note that
\{\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{u}_{t}\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{\top}\right)\}_{t=1}^{T}
is a sequence of martingale differences, the Lindeberg’s condition can be verified as
\|\mathbf{u}_{t}\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{\top}\|_{\delta/2}\leq\|\mathbf{u}_{t}\|_{\delta}\|\mathbf{w}_{t-1}\|_{\delta}
for some
\delta>4
and the convergence of conditional variance can be verified by Lemma
A.2
(2). We then have

\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{vec}\left(\mathbf{u}_{t}\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{\top}\right)\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)\to_{D}N\left(\mathbf{0},\widetilde{v}_{2l}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{w}}(\tau)\otimes\bm{\Omega}(\tau)\right)

and thus
\mathbf{J}_{T,42}=O_{P}(1)
. Next, consider
\mathbf{J}_{T,41}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{J}_{T,41}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\begin{matrix}\frac{1}{T\sqrt{h}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{u}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top}\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}(\tau)\bm{\xi}_{T}(\tau)\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)&\frac{1}{Th}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{u}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top}\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}(\tau)\bm{\xi}_{T,\perp}(\tau)\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)\\
\end{matrix}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\mathbf{J}_{T,411},\mathbf{J}_{T,412}\right].

For
\mathbf{J}_{T,411}
, similar to the proof of Lemma
A.1
(1), by the continuous mapping theory, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{J}_{T,411}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{u}_{t}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\mathbf{y}_{\delta_{T}}^{\top}\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}(\tau)\bm{\xi}_{T}(\tau)\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{u}_{t}\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\left(\mathbf{y}_{t-1}-\mathbf{y}_{\delta_{T}}\right)^{\top}\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}(\tau)\bm{\xi}_{T}(\tau)\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{u}_{t}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\mathbf{y}_{\delta_{T}}^{\top}\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}(\tau)\bm{\xi}_{T}(\tau)\right)+O_{P}(\sqrt{h})

\displaystyle\to_{D}

\displaystyle\sqrt{2\mathbf{q}(\tau)^{\top}\mathbf{q}(\tau)}\int_{-1}^{1}u^{l}K(u)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{W}_{d}^{*}((u+1)/2,\bm{\Omega}(\tau)).

Similarly, for
\mathbf{J}_{T,412}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{J}_{T,412}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\sqrt{2Th}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{u}_{t}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2Th}}\left(\mathbf{y}_{t-1}-\mathbf{y}_{\delta_{T}}\right)^{\top}\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}(\tau)\bm{\xi}_{T,\perp}(\tau)\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)

\displaystyle\to_{D}

\displaystyle\left(\bm{\xi}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\int_{-1}^{1}\mathbf{W}_{d}^{*}((u+1)/2,\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\alpha}}(\tau))u^{l}K(u)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{W}_{d}^{*,\top}((u+1)/2,\bm{\Omega}(\tau))\right)^{\top}.

Therefore, we have
\mathbf{J}_{T,41}=O_{P}(1)
. Hence, by Lemma
A.1
(6), we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{J}_{T,4}=\left[O_{P}(1),\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{u}_{t}\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{\top}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right),O_{P}(1),\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{u}_{t}\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{\top}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\times\left[\begin{matrix}O_{P}(1)&O_{P}(1/\sqrt{Th})\\
O_{P}(1/\sqrt{Th})&\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{w}}^{-1}(\tau)+O_{P}(h^{2}+1/\sqrt{Th})\\
O_{P}(1)&O_{P}(1/\sqrt{Th})\\
O_{P}(1/\sqrt{Th})&O_{P}(h+1/\sqrt{Th})\\
\end{matrix}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[O_{P}(1),\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{u}_{t}\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{w}}^{-1}(\tau)K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)\right]+o_{P}(1).

Combining the above derivations, as
Th^{5}=O(1)
, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{Th}\mathrm{vec}\left(\left([\widehat{\bm{\Pi}}(\tau),\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}(\tau)]-[\bm{\Pi}(\tau),\bm{\Gamma}(\tau)]-\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{c}_{2}h^{2}[\bm{\Pi}^{(2)}(\tau),\bm{\Gamma}^{(2)}(\tau)]\right)\mathbf{Q}^{*,-1}(\tau)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{Q}^{*,-1,\top}(\tau)\otimes\mathbf{I}_{d}\right)\sqrt{Th}\left(\mathrm{vec}\left([\widehat{\bm{\Pi}}(\tau),\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}(\tau)]-[\bm{\Pi}(\tau),\bm{\Gamma}(\tau)]-\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{c}_{2}h^{2}[\bm{\Pi}^{(2)}(\tau),\bm{\Gamma}^{(2)}(\tau)]\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{vec}\left[\begin{matrix}\mathbf{0}_{d\times(d-r_{0})}&\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{u}_{t}\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{\top}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{w}}^{-1}(\tau)\end{matrix}\right]+o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle\to_{D}

\displaystyle\left[\begin{matrix}\mathbf{0}_{d(d-r_{0})\times 1}\\
N\left(\mathbf{0},\widetilde{v}_{0}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{w}}^{-1}(\tau)\otimes\bm{\Omega}(\tau)\right)\end{matrix}\right],

which implies

\displaystyle\sqrt{Th}\mathrm{vec}\left([\widehat{\bm{\Pi}}(\tau),\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}(\tau)]-[\bm{\Pi}(\tau),\bm{\Gamma}(\tau)]-\frac{1}{2}h^{2}\widetilde{c}_{2}[\bm{\Pi}^{(2)}(\tau),\bm{\Gamma}^{(2)}(\tau)]\right)

\displaystyle\to_{D}

\displaystyle N\left(\mathbf{0},\widetilde{v}_{0}\left(\left[\begin{matrix}\bm{\beta}&\mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{I}_{d(p_{0}-1)}\\
\end{matrix}\right]\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{w}}^{-1}(\tau)\left[\begin{matrix}\bm{\beta}^{\top}&\mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{I}_{d(p_{0}-1)}\\
\end{matrix}\right]\right)\otimes\bm{\Omega}(\tau)\right).

(2). Applying the uniform convergence results in Lemmas
A.2
and
A.4
to terms
\mathbf{J}_{T,1}
–
\mathbf{J}_{T,4}
, which are defined in the proof of part (1), we have

\sup_{\tau\in[0,1]}\left|[\widehat{\bm{\Pi}}(\tau),\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}(\tau)]-[\bm{\Pi}(\tau),\bm{\Gamma}(\tau)]\right|=O_{P}\left(h^{2}+\sqrt{\log T/(Th)}\right).

(3). Consider
\widehat{\bm{\Omega}}(\tau)
first. Note that by the proof of part (1) and the uniform convergence results in Lemmas
A.2
and
A.4
, we have

\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq 1}\left|\left([\widehat{\bm{\Pi}}(\tau),\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}(\tau)]-[\bm{\Pi}(\tau),\bm{\Gamma}(\tau)]\right)\mathbf{Q}^{*,-1}(\tau)\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*}(\tau)\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*}\right|=O_{P}\left([\log T,\sqrt{Th}h^{2}+\sqrt{\log T}]\right).

Note that

\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{t}=\mathbf{u}_{t}+\left(\bm{\Pi}(\tau_{t})-\widehat{\bm{\Pi}}(\tau_{t})\right)\mathbf{y}_{t-1}+\left(\bm{\Gamma}(\tau_{t})-\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}(\tau_{t})\right)\Delta\mathbf{x}_{t-1},

which implies that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\mathbf{u}_{t}-\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{t}\right)\mathbf{u}_{t}^{\top}w_{t}(\tau)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left([\widehat{\bm{\Pi}}(\tau_{t}),\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}(\tau_{t})]-[\bm{\Pi}(\tau_{t}),\bm{\Gamma}(\tau_{t})]\right)\mathbf{h}_{t-1}\mathbf{u}_{t}^{\top}w_{t}(\tau)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left([\widehat{\bm{\Pi}}(\tau_{t}),\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}(\tau_{t})]-[\bm{\Pi}(\tau_{t}),\bm{\Gamma}(\tau_{t})]\right)\mathbf{Q}^{*,-1}(\tau)\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*}(\tau)\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*}\left[\begin{matrix}\frac{1}{T\sqrt{h}}\bm{\xi}_{T}^{\top}(\tau_{t})\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\\
\frac{1}{Th}\bm{\xi}_{T,\perp}^{\top}(\tau_{t})\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau_{t})(\mathbf{y}_{t-1}-\mathbf{y}_{\delta_{T}})\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\bm{\beta}^{\top}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\Delta\mathbf{x}_{t-1}\end{matrix}\right]\mathbf{u}_{t}^{\top}w_{t}(\tau)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}(h^{2}+\sqrt{\log T/(Th)})

uniformly over
\tau\in[0,1]
. Similarly, it is easy to show that

\sup_{\tau\in[0,1]}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\mathbf{u}_{t}-\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{t}\right)\left(\mathbf{u}_{t}-\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{t}\right)^{\top}w_{t}(\tau)\right|=O_{P}\left(h^{4}+\log T/(Th)\right).

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in[0,1]}\left|\widehat{\bm{\Omega}}(\tau)-\bm{\Omega}(\tau)\right|=\sup_{\tau\in[0,1]}\left|T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{u}_{t}\mathbf{u}_{t}^{\top}w_{t}\left(\tau\right)-\bm{\Omega}(\tau)\right|+O_{P}\left(h^{2}+\sqrt{\log T/(Th)}\right).

In addition, by using similar arguments to the proof of Lemma
A.2
, we have

\sup_{\tau\in[0,1]}\left|T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{u}_{t}\mathbf{u}_{t}^{\top}w_{t}\left(\tau\right)-\bm{\Omega}(\tau)\right|=O_{P}\left(h^{2}+\sqrt{\log T/(Th)}\right).

Next, consider
\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)\right]\mathbf{S}_{T,0}^{+}(\tau)
. By Lemmas
A.2
and
A.4
, we have uniformly over
\tau\in[h,1-h]
,

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)\right]\mathbf{S}_{T,0}^{+}(\tau)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Th(\widetilde{c}_{0}+O(1/(Th)))\mathbf{Q}^{*,\top}(\tau)\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*}(\tau)\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*,-1}

\displaystyle\left[\begin{matrix}\bm{\Delta}_{0}^{-1}(\tau)+o_{P}(1)&O_{P}(\sqrt{\log T/(Th)})&\\
O_{P}(\sqrt{\log T/(Th)})&\widetilde{c}_{0}^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{w}}^{-1}(\tau)+O_{P}(h^{2}+\sqrt{\log T/(Th)})\\
\end{matrix}\right]\cdot\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*,-1}\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*,\top}(\tau)\mathbf{Q}^{*}(\tau)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{Q}^{*,\top}(\tau)\left[\begin{matrix}\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{0}&\\
\mathbf{0}&\widetilde{c}_{0}^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{w}}^{-1}(\tau)\\
\end{matrix}\right]\mathbf{Q}^{*}(\tau)+O_{P}(h^{2}+\sqrt{\log T/(Th)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\begin{matrix}\bm{\beta}&\mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{I}_{d(p_{0}-1)}\\
\end{matrix}\right]\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{w}}^{-1}(\tau)\left[\begin{matrix}\bm{\beta}^{\top}&\mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{I}_{d(p_{0}-1)}\\
\end{matrix}\right]+O_{P}(h^{2}+\sqrt{\log T/(Th)}).

The proof is now completed.

∎

Proof of Theorem
2.2
.

Note that
\widetilde{\mathbf{r}}_{t}(\widehat{\bm{\alpha}})=\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{t}^{\top}(\widehat{\bm{\alpha}})\mathrm{vec}\left(\bm{\beta}^{*,\top}\right)+\mathbf{u}_{t}^{*}
, where

\mathbf{u}_{t}^{*}=\mathbf{u}_{t}+\left(\bm{\alpha}(\tau_{t})-\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}(\tau_{t})\right)\bm{\beta}^{\top}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}+\left(\bm{\Pi}(\tau_{t})-\widehat{\bm{\Pi}}(\tau_{t},\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}\bm{\beta}^{\top})\right)\Delta\mathbf{x}_{t-1}

and

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}(\tau,\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}\bm{\beta}^{\top})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{T}(\Delta\mathbf{y}_{s}-\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}(\tau_{s})\bm{\beta}^{\top}\mathbf{y}_{s-1})\Delta\mathbf{x}_{s-1}^{*,\top}K\left(\frac{\tau_{s}-\tau}{h}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(\sum_{s=1}^{T}\Delta\mathbf{x}_{s-1}^{*}\Delta\mathbf{x}_{s-1}^{*,\top}K\left(\frac{\tau_{s}-\tau}{h}\right)\right)^{-1}\left[\begin{matrix}\mathbf{I}_{d(p_{0}-1)}\\
\mathbf{0}_{d(p_{0}-1)}\end{matrix}\right],

which implies that

\displaystyle T\mathrm{vec}\left[\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{*,\top}-\bm{\beta}^{*,\top}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{t}(\widehat{\bm{\alpha}})\widehat{\bm{\Omega}}^{-1}(\tau_{t})\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{t}^{\top}(\widehat{\bm{\alpha}})\right)^{-1}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{t}(\widehat{\bm{\alpha}})\widehat{\bm{\Omega}}^{-1}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{u}_{t}^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{t}(\widehat{\bm{\alpha}})\widehat{\bm{\Omega}}^{-1}(\tau_{t})\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{t}^{\top}(\widehat{\bm{\alpha}})\right)^{-1}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{t}(\widehat{\bm{\alpha}})\widehat{\bm{\Omega}}^{-1}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{u}_{t}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{t}(\widehat{\bm{\alpha}})\widehat{\bm{\Omega}}^{-1}(\tau_{t})\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{t}^{\top}(\widehat{\bm{\alpha}})\right)^{-1}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{t}(\widehat{\bm{\alpha}})\widehat{\bm{\Omega}}^{-1}(\tau_{t})\left(\bm{\alpha}(\tau_{t})-\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}(\tau_{t})\right)\bm{\beta}^{\top}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{t}(\widehat{\bm{\alpha}})\widehat{\bm{\Omega}}^{-1}(\tau_{t})\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{t}^{\top}(\widehat{\bm{\alpha}})\right)^{-1}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}_{t}(\widehat{\bm{\alpha}})\widehat{\bm{\Omega}}^{-1}(\tau_{t})\left(\bm{\Pi}(\tau_{t})-\widehat{\bm{\Pi}}(\tau_{t},\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}\bm{\beta}^{\top})\right)\Delta\mathbf{x}_{t-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{J}_{T,5}+\mathbf{J}_{T,6}+\mathbf{J}_{T,7}.

By Lemma
A.5
,
\mathbf{J}_{T,6}
and
\mathbf{J}_{T,7}
are both
o_{P}(1)
. Next, consider
\mathbf{J}_{T,5}
. By Lemma
A.5
, we have

\mathbf{J}_{T,5}\to_{D}\left(\int_{0}^{1}\mathbf{W}_{d-r_{0}}(u)\mathbf{W}_{d-r_{0}}^{\top}(u)\otimes\bm{\alpha}^{\top}(u)\bm{\Omega}^{-1}(u)\bm{\alpha}(u)\mathrm{d}u\right)^{-1}\int_{0}^{1}\mathbf{W}_{d-r_{0}}(u)\otimes\mathrm{d}\mathbf{W}_{r_{0}}(u),

where
\mathbf{W}_{d-r_{0}}(u)
and
\mathbf{W}_{r_{0}}(u)
are mutually independent.

Since
\mathbf{W}_{d-r_{0}}(u)
and
\mathbf{W}_{r_{0}}(u)
are mutually independent, the conditional distribution of
\mathbf{J}_{T,5}
, given
\mathbf{W}_{d-r_{0}}(u)
, is

\mathbf{J}_{T,5}\to_{D}N\left(\mathbf{0},\left(\int_{0}^{1}\mathbf{W}_{d-r_{0}}(u)\mathbf{W}_{d-r_{0}}^{\top}(u)\otimes\bm{\alpha}^{\top}(u)\bm{\Omega}^{-1}(u)\bm{\alpha}(u)\mathrm{d}u\right)^{-1}\right),

which implies that

\left(\frac{1}{T^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{(2)}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{(2),\top}\otimes\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}^{\top}(\tau_{t})\widehat{\bm{\Omega}}^{-1}(\tau_{t})\widehat{\bm{\alpha}}(\tau_{t})\right)^{1/2}\mathbf{J}_{T,1}\to_{D}N\left(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}_{(d-r_{0})r_{0}}\right).

The proof is now completed.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.3
.

We need to prove that
\lim_{T\to\infty}\Pr\left(\text{IC}(p)<\text{IC}(p_{0})\right)=0
for all
p\neq p_{0}
and
p\leq P
. Note that

\text{IC}(p)-\text{IC}(p_{0})=\log[\text{RSS}(p)/\text{RSS}(p_{0})]+(p-p_{0})\chi_{T}.

(i) For
p<p_{0}
, Lemma
A.6
implies that
\text{RSS}(p)/\text{RSS}(p_{0})>1+\nu
for some
\nu>0
with large probability for all large
T
. Thus,
\log[\text{RSS}(p)/\text{RSS}(p_{0})]\geq\nu/2
for large
T
. Because
\chi_{T}\to 0
, we have
\text{IC}(p)-\text{IC}(p_{0})\geq\nu/2-(p_{0}-p)\chi_{T}\geq\nu/3
for large
T
with large probability. Thus
\Pr\left(\text{IC}(p)<\text{IC}(p_{0})\right)\to 0
for
p<p_{0}
.

(ii) We then consider
p>p_{0}
. Lemma
A.6
implies that
\text{RSS}(p)/\text{RSS}(p_{0})=1+O_{P}(c_{T}^{2})
with
c_{T}=h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(T)}{Th}}
. Hence,
\log[\text{RSS}(p)/\text{RSS}(p_{0})]=O_{P}(c_{T}^{2})
. Because
(p-p_{0})\chi_{T}\geq\chi_{T}
, which converges to zero at a slower rate than
c_{T}^{2}
, it implies that

\Pr\left(\text{IC}(p)<\text{IC}(p_{0})\right)\leq\Pr\left(\log[\text{RSS}(p)/\text{RSS}(p_{0})]+\chi_{T}<0\right)\to 0.

The proof is now completed. ∎

Proof of Theorem
2.4
.

By the proof of Theorem
2.1
and using the uniform convergence results in Lemmas
A.2
and
A.4
, we have uniformly over
\tau\in[0,1]

\displaystyle\left[\widehat{\bm{\Pi}}(\tau)-{\bm{\Pi}}(\tau),\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}(\tau)-{\bm{\Gamma}}(\tau)\right]\mathbf{Q}^{*,-1}(\tau)\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{*}(\tau)\mathbf{D}_{T}^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\mathbf{0}_{d\times(d-r_{0})},\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{Th^{5}}\widetilde{c}_{2}(\tau)\bm{\alpha}^{(2)}(\tau),\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{Th^{5}}\widetilde{c}_{2}(\tau)\bm{\Gamma}^{(2)}(\tau)\right]+O_{P}([\log T,\sqrt{\log T}]),

where
\tilde{c}_{k}(\tau)=\int_{-\tau/h}^{(1-\tau)/h}u^{k}K(u)\mathrm{d}u
. Hence, we have

\int_{0}^{1}\widehat{\bm{\Pi}}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau-\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\alpha}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\bm{\beta}^{\top}=\frac{1}{2}h^{2}\int_{0}^{1}\tilde{c}_{2}(\tau)\bm{\alpha}^{(2)}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\bm{\beta}^{\top}+\left[\begin{matrix}O_{P}\left(\log T/(Th)\right)\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\\
O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\log T/(Th)}\right)\bm{\beta}^{\top}\end{matrix}\right],

which implies that
\bm{\beta}_{\perp}^{\top}\int_{0}^{1}\widehat{\bm{\Pi}}^{\top}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau=O_{P}\left(\log T/(Th)\right)
and

\bm{\beta}^{\top}\int_{0}^{1}\widehat{\bm{\Pi}}^{\top}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau=\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\beta}\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\alpha}^{\top}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau+O_{P}\left(h^{2}+\sqrt{\log T/(Th)}\right).

Let
\bm{\beta}_{0}=[\bm{\beta}(\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\beta})^{-1/2},\bm{\beta}_{\perp}]^{\top}
. Since
\bm{\beta}_{0}^{\top}\bm{\beta}_{0}=\mathbf{I}_{d}
, by the unitary invariance properties of singular values, we have

\sigma_{j}\left(\bm{\beta}_{0}\int_{0}^{1}\widehat{\bm{\Pi}}^{\top}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\right)=\sigma_{j}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{S}}\int_{0}^{1}\widehat{\bm{\Pi}}^{\top}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\right)=\sigma_{j}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}})

for all
j=1,\ldots,d
. Since
(\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\beta})^{-1/2}\bm{\beta}^{\top}\int_{0}^{1}\widehat{\bm{\Pi}}^{\top}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau=(\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\beta})^{1/2}\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\alpha}^{\top}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau+O_{P}\left(h^{2}+\sqrt{\log T/(Th)}\right)
, by matrix perturbation theory, we have
\sigma_{j}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}\right)-\sigma_{j}\left((\bm{\beta}^{\top}\bm{\beta})^{1/2}\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\alpha}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\right)=O_{P}\left(h^{2}+\sqrt{\log T/(Th)}\right)
for
j=1,\ldots,r_{0}
. Similarly, we have
\sigma_{j}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}\right)=O_{P}\left(\log T/(Th)\right)
for
j=r_{0}+1,\ldots,d
.

By the column-pivoting step in the QR decomposition, we can conclude that

\sigma_{r_{0}}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}})\leq\widehat{\mu}_{k}=\sqrt{\sum_{j=k}^{d}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{2}(k,j)}\leq\sigma_{1}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}})\hskip 9.24994pt\text{for}\hskip 9.24994ptk=1,\ldots,r_{0}

and

\widehat{\mu}_{k}=\sqrt{\sum_{j=k}^{d}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{2}(k,j)}=O_{P}(\log T/(Th))\hskip 9.24994pt\text{for}\hskip 9.24994ptk=r_{0}+1,\ldots,d.

When
r_{0}=0
, we have
\widehat{\mu}_{k}=O_{P}(\log T/(Th))
for
k=1,\ldots,d
, which implies that
\widehat{\mu}_{0}/\widehat{\mu}_{1}\to\infty
, while
\widehat{\mu}_{r}/\widehat{\mu}_{r+1}=1
for
r\geq 1
with probability approaching to 1. Similarly, when
0<r_{0}<d
,
\widehat{\mu}_{r}/\widehat{\mu}_{r+1}\asymp 1
for
r=1,\ldots,r_{0}-1
,
\widehat{\mu}_{r_{0}}/\widehat{\mu}_{r_{0}+1}\to\infty
, and
\widehat{\mu}_{r}/\widehat{\mu}_{r+1}=1
for
r\geq r_{0}+1
with probability approaching to 1. The proof is now completed.

∎

Proof of Theorem
2.5
.

(1). By part (2) of Theorem
2.1
, we have

\sup_{\tau\in[0,1]}\left|[\widehat{\bm{\Pi}}(\tau),\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}(\tau)]-[\bm{\Pi}(\tau),\bm{\Gamma}(\tau)]\right|=O_{P}\left(h^{2}+\sqrt{\log T/(Th)}\right)

and thus by
Th^{6}\to 0
, we have

\sqrt{T}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{c}}-\mathbf{c}\right)=\sqrt{T}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{C}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{b}}(\tau_{t})-\mathbf{b}(\tau_{t})\right)=\sqrt{T}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=\lfloor Th\rfloor+1}^{\lfloor T(1-h)\rfloor}\mathbf{C}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{b}}(\tau_{t})-\mathbf{b}(\tau_{t})\right)+o_{P}(1).

In addition, by the proof of part(1) of Theorem
2.1
and the uniform convergence results in Lemmas
A.2
and
A.4
, we have uniformly over
\tau\in[h,1-h]
,

\displaystyle[\widehat{\bm{\Pi}}(\tau)-\bm{\Pi}(\tau),\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}(\tau)-\bm{\Gamma}(\tau)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}h^{2}\widetilde{c}_{2}[\bm{\alpha}^{(2)}(\tau)\bm{\beta}^{\top},\bm{\Gamma}^{(2)}(\tau)]+O(h^{3})+O_{P}\left(h^{2}\sqrt{\log T/(Th)}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{Th}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{u}_{t}\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{w}}^{-1}(\tau)K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)\left[\begin{matrix}\bm{\beta}^{\top}&\mathbf{0}_{r_{0}\times d(p_{0}-1)}\\
\mathbf{0}_{d(p_{0}-1)\times r_{0}}&\mathbf{I}_{d(p_{0}-1)}\\
\end{matrix}\right]+O_{P}\left(\log T/(Th)\right).

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{b}}(\tau)-\mathbf{b}(\tau)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}h^{2}\widetilde{c}_{2}\mathbf{b}^{(2)}(\tau)+\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{w}}^{-1}(\tau)\otimes\mathbf{I}_{d}\left(\frac{1}{Th}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{w}_{t-1}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{t}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+O_{P}(h^{2}\sqrt{\log T/(Th)})+O(h^{3})+O_{P}(\log T/(Th))

uniformly over
\tau\in[h,1-h]
.

Combining the above two results, as
Th^{6}\to 0
and
Th^{2}/(\log T)^{2}\to\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{T}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{c}}-\mathbf{c}-\frac{1}{2}h^{2}\widetilde{c}_{2}\int_{0}^{1}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{b}^{(2)}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1}{Th}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{w}}^{-1}(\tau_{s})\otimes\mathbf{I}_{d}K\left(\frac{\tau_{s}-\tau_{t}}{h}\right)\right)\mathbf{w}_{t-1}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{t}+o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{w}}^{-1}(\tau_{t})\otimes\mathbf{I}_{d}\right)\mathbf{w}_{t-1}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{t}+o_{P}(1)

\displaystyle\to_{D}

\displaystyle N\left(\bm{0},\int_{0}^{1}\mathbf{C}\left(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{w}}^{-1}(\tau)\otimes\mathbf{\Omega}(\tau)\right)\mathbf{C}^{\top}\mathrm{d}\tau\right)

by the conventional martingale central limit theory. Note that the Lindeberg’s condition can be verified as
\|\mathbf{w}_{t-1}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{t}\|_{\delta/2}\leq\|\mathbf{u}_{t}\|_{\delta}\|\mathbf{w}_{t-1}\|_{\delta}
for some
\delta>4
and the convergence of conditional variance can be verified by Lemma
A.2
(2).

(2). By part (1), we have
\sup_{\tau\in[0,1]}\|\widehat{\mathbf{b}}(\tau)-\mathbf{b}(\tau)\|=O_{P}\left(h^{2}+\sqrt{\log T/(Th)}\right)
. Then we can conclude that as
Th^{11/2}=o(1)
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t\in\mathcal{B}_{T}}\left[\mathbf{C}\widehat{\mathbf{b}}(\tau_{t})-\mathbf{c}\right]^{\top}\mathbf{H}(\tau_{t})\left[\mathbf{C}\widehat{\mathbf{b}}(\tau_{t})-\mathbf{c}\right]=T^{-1}\times(Th)\times O_{P}\left(\log T/(Th)+h^{4}\right)

(A.4.1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}\left(\log T/T+h^{5}\right)=o_{P}\left(T^{-1}h^{-1/2}\right),

where
\mathcal{B}_{T}=\{1,\ldots,\lfloor Th\rfloor\}\cup\{\lfloor T(1-h)\rfloor,\ldots,T\}
.

In addition, by Lemma
A.2
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in[h,1-h]}\left|\frac{1}{Th}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{w}_{t-1}\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{\top}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)-\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{w}}(\tau)\right|=O_{P}\left(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log T}{Th}}\right),

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in[0,1]}\left|\mathbf{R}_{T}(\tau)\right|=O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log T}{Th}}\right),

(A.4.2)

where
\mathbf{R}_{T}(\tau)=\frac{1}{Th}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{W}_{t-1}\mathbf{u}_{t}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)
and
\mathbf{W}_{t}=\mathbf{w}_{t}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{d}
. Hence, under the null hypothesis, we have the following Bahadur representation

\displaystyle\sup_{[h,1-h]}\left|\mathbf{C}\widehat{\mathbf{b}}(\tau)-\mathbf{c}-\mathbf{C}\left(\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{w}}^{-1}(\tau)\otimes\mathbf{I}_{d}\right)\mathbf{R}_{T}(\tau)\right|=O_{P}(\rho_{T}^{2})

(A.4.3)

with
\rho_{T}=h^{2}+\sqrt{{\log T}/{(Th)}}
.

By (
A.4.1
)-(
A.4.3
) and
Th^{11/2}\to 0
, under the null we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left[\mathbf{C}\widehat{\mathbf{b}}(\tau_{t})-\mathbf{c}\right]^{\top}\mathbf{H}(\tau_{t})\left[\mathbf{C}\widehat{\mathbf{b}}(\tau_{t})-\mathbf{c}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=\lfloor Th\rfloor+1}^{\lfloor T(1-h)\rfloor}\mathbf{R}_{T}^{\top}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{H}_{0}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{R}_{T}(\tau_{t})+O_{P}\left(\log T/T+h^{5}\right)+O_{P}\left(\rho_{T}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log T}{Th}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{R}_{T}^{\top}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{H}_{0}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{R}_{T}(\tau_{t})+o_{P}(T^{-1}h^{-1/2}),

where
\mathbf{H}_{0}(\tau)=\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{W}}^{-1}(\tau)\mathbf{C}^{\top}\mathbf{H}(\tau)\mathbf{C}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{W}}^{-1}(\tau)
and
\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{W}}(\tau)=\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{w}}(\tau)\otimes\mathbf{I}_{d}
.

Consider
\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{R}_{T}^{\top}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{H}_{0}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{R}_{T}(\tau_{t})
, and write

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{R}_{T}^{\top}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{H}_{0}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{R}_{T}(\tau_{t})=\frac{1}{T^{2}h^{2}}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathbf{u}_{s}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{s-1}^{\top}\left\{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{H}_{0}(\tau_{t})K^{2}\left(\frac{\tau_{s}-\tau_{t}}{h}\right)\right\}\mathbf{W}_{s-1}\mathbf{u}_{s}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{T^{2}h^{2}}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\sum_{v=1,\neq s}^{T}\mathbf{u}_{s}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{s-1}^{\top}\left\{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{H}_{0}(\tau_{t})K\left(\frac{\tau_{s}-\tau_{t}}{h}\right)K\left(\frac{\tau_{v}-\tau_{t}}{h}\right)\right\}\mathbf{W}_{v-1}\mathbf{u}_{v}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle J_{T,8}+J_{T,9},

where the definitions of
J_{T,8}
and
J_{T,9}
should be obvious.

Consider
J_{T,8}
. We first introduce some additional notation to facilitate the development. Let
A_{Q}=\widetilde{v}_{0}\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left\{\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\Sigma}_{Q}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\right\}
and
B_{Q}=4C_{B}\cdot\mathrm{tr}\left\{\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\Sigma}_{Q}^{2}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\right\}
, where

\bm{\Sigma}_{Q}(\tau)=\mathbf{H}(\tau)^{1/2}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{V}_{\mathbf{b}}(\tau)\mathbf{C}^{\top}\mathbf{H}(\tau)^{1/2}.

Simple algebra shows that

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{t-1}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{W}}^{-1}(\tau)\mathbf{C}^{\top}\mathbf{H}(\tau)\mathbf{C}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{W}}^{-1}(\tau)\mathbf{W}_{t-1}\mathbf{u}_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}\left\{\left[\left(\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{w}}^{-1}(\tau)\mathbf{w}_{t-1}\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{w}}^{-1}(\tau)\right)\otimes\mathbf{u}_{t}\mathbf{u}_{t}^{\top}\right]\cdot\mathbf{C}^{\top}\mathbf{H}(\tau)\mathbf{C}\right\},

which implies that

\displaystyle J_{T,8}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{v}_{0}\frac{1}{T^{2}h}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathbf{u}_{s}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{s-1}^{\top}\left[\mathbf{H}_{0}(\tau_{s})+O(h)\right]\mathbf{W}_{s-1}\mathbf{u}_{s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{v}_{0}\frac{1}{T^{2}h}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathrm{tr}\left\{\left[\left(\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{w}}^{-1}(\tau_{s})\mathbf{w}_{s-1}\mathbf{w}_{s-1}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{w}}^{-1}(\tau_{s})\right)\otimes\mathbf{u}_{s}\mathbf{u}_{s}^{\top}\right]\cdot\mathbf{C}^{\top}\mathbf{H}(\tau_{s})\mathbf{C}\right\}+O_{P}(T^{-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{v}_{0}\frac{1}{T^{2}h}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{tr}\left\{\left[\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{w}}^{-1}(\tau_{t})\otimes\bm{\Omega}(\tau_{t})\right]\cdot\mathbf{C}^{\top}\mathbf{H}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{C}\right\}+O_{P}(T^{-1}+T^{-3/2}h^{-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(Th)^{-1}A_{Q}+o_{P}\left(T^{-1}h^{-1/2}\right).

Consider
J_{T,9}
. Let
w_{s,v}=\frac{1}{T\sqrt{h}}\int_{-1}^{1}K\left(u\right)K\left(u+\frac{s-v}{Th}\right)\mathrm{d}u
. Since

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{H}_{0}(\tau_{t})K\left(\frac{\tau_{s}-\tau_{t}}{h}\right)K\left(\frac{\tau_{v}-\tau_{t}}{h}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h\int_{-1}^{1}\mathbf{H}_{0}(\tau_{s}+uh)K\left(u\right)K\left(u+\frac{s-v}{Th}\right)\mathrm{d}u+O(1/(Th)),

we have

T\sqrt{h}J_{T,9}=2\sum_{s=2}^{T}\sum_{v=1}^{s-1}\mathbf{u}_{s}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{s-1}^{\top}\mathbf{H}_{0}(\tau_{s})\mathbf{W}_{v-1}\mathbf{u}_{v}w_{s,v}(1+o(1))=2\widetilde{U}+o_{P}(1),

where the definition of
\widetilde{U}
is obvious. By Lemma
A.7
, we have

\widetilde{U}\to_{D}N\left(0,\sigma_{\widetilde{U}}^{2}\right),

where
\sigma_{\widetilde{U}}^{2}=\lim_{T\to\infty}\sum_{s=2}^{T}\mathrm{tr}\left\{E\left(\mathbf{H}_{0}^{\top}(\tau_{s})\mathbf{W}_{s-1}\mathbf{u}_{s}\mathbf{u}_{s}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{s-1}^{\top}\mathbf{H}_{0}(\tau_{s})\right)E\left(\sum_{v=1}^{s-1}\mathbf{W}_{v-1}\mathbf{u}_{v}\mathbf{u}_{v}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{v-1}^{\top}\right)w_{s,v}^{2}\right\}
. We then show that
\sigma_{\widetilde{U}}^{2}=C_{B}\mathrm{tr}\left\{\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\Sigma}_{Q}(\tau)^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau\right\}
. Let
\mathbf{V}_{1}(\tau)=\mathbf{H}_{0}(\tau)\mathbf{V}_{2}(\tau)\mathbf{H}_{0}(\tau)
and
\mathbf{V}_{2}(\tau)=\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{w}}(\tau)\otimes\bm{\Omega}(\tau)
. Write

\displaystyle\sum_{t=2}^{T}E\left(\mathbf{H}_{0}^{\top}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{W}_{t-1}\bm{\eta}_{t}\mathbf{u}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{t-1}^{\top}\mathbf{H}_{0}(\tau_{t})\right)E\left(\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}\mathbf{W}_{s-1}\mathbf{u}_{s}\mathbf{u}_{s}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{s-1}^{\top}\right)w_{s,t}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=2}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}\mathbf{V}_{1}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{V}_{2}(\tau_{s})w_{s,t}^{2}=\frac{1}{T^{2}h}\sum_{t=2}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}\mathbf{V}_{1}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{V}_{2}(\tau_{s})\left[\int_{-1}^{1}K\left(u\right)K\left(u+\frac{t-s}{Th}\right)\mathrm{d}u\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T^{2}h}\sum_{s=1}^{T-1}\sum_{j=1}^{T-s}\mathbf{V}_{1}(\tau_{s}+j/T)\mathbf{V}_{2}(\tau_{s})\left[\int_{-1}^{1}K\left(u\right)K\left(u+\frac{t-s}{Th}\right)\mathrm{d}u\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T^{2}h}\sum_{s=1}^{T-1}\sum_{j=1}^{T-s}\mathbf{V}_{1}(\tau_{s}+j/T)\mathbf{V}_{2}(\tau_{s})\left[\int_{-1}^{1}K\left(u\right)K\left(u+\frac{j}{Th}\right)\mathrm{d}u\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T^{2}h}\sum_{s=1}^{T-1}\sum_{j=1}^{T-s}\mathbf{V}_{1}(\tau_{s})\mathbf{V}_{2}(\tau_{s})\left[\int_{-1}^{1}K\left(u\right)K\left(u+\frac{j}{Th}\right)\mathrm{d}u\right]^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{T^{2}h}\sum_{s=1}^{T-1}\sum_{j=1}^{T-s}O(j/T)\mathbf{V}_{2}(\tau_{s})\left[\int_{-1}^{1}K\left(u\right)K\left(u+\frac{j}{Th}\right)\mathrm{d}u\right]^{2}:=J_{T,9}+J_{T,10},

where the definitions of
J_{T,9}
and
J_{T,10}
are obvious.

It is easy to verify
\mathrm{tr}\left\{J_{T,9}\right\}\to C_{B}\mathrm{tr}\left\{\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\Sigma}_{Q}(\tau)^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau\right\}
. For
J_{T,10}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|J_{T,10}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M\frac{1}{Th}\sum_{j=1}^{T}j/T\left[\int_{-1}^{1}K\left(u\right)K\left(u+\frac{j}{Th}\right)\mathrm{d}u\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Mh\int_{0}^{2}v\left[\int_{-1}^{1}K\left(u\right)K\left(u+v\right)\mathrm{d}u\right]^{2}\mathrm{d}v+o(1)=o(1).

Combining the above results, we have proved

\displaystyle T\sqrt{h}\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{R}_{T}^{\top}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{H}_{0}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{R}_{T}(\tau_{t})-(Th)^{-1}A_{Q}\right]\to_{D}N\left(0,B_{Q}\right).

Note that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left[\mathbf{C}\widehat{\mathbf{b}}(\tau_{t})-\widehat{\mathbf{c}}\right]^{\top}\mathbf{H}(\tau_{t})\left[\mathbf{C}\widehat{\mathbf{b}}(\tau_{t})-\widehat{\mathbf{c}}\right]-\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left[\mathbf{C}\widehat{\mathbf{b}}(\tau_{t})-\mathbf{c}\right]^{\top}\mathbf{H}(\tau_{t})\left[\mathbf{C}\widehat{\mathbf{b}}(\tau_{t})-\mathbf{c}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{\mathbf{c}}-\mathbf{c}\right)^{\top}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{H}(\tau_{t})\left(\widehat{\mathbf{c}}-\mathbf{c}\right)-2\left(\widehat{\mathbf{c}}-\mathbf{c}\right)^{\top}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{H}(\tau_{t})\left(\mathbf{C}\widehat{\mathbf{b}}(\tau_{t})-\mathbf{c}\right):=J_{T,11}-2J_{T,12}.

Since
\widehat{\mathbf{c}}=\mathbf{c}+O_{P}\left(T^{-1/2}\right)
by part (1), we have
J_{T,11}=O_{P}(T^{-1})=o_{P}(T^{-1}h^{-1/2})
. For
J_{T,12}
, by (
A.4.1
) and (
A.4.3
), we have

\displaystyle J_{T,12}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{\mathbf{c}}-\mathbf{c}\right)^{\top}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=\lfloor Th\rfloor+1}^{\lfloor T(1-h)\rfloor}\mathbf{H}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{C}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{W}}^{-1}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{R}_{T}(\tau_{t})+o_{P}\left(T^{-1}h^{-1/2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}(T^{-1})+o_{P}\left(T^{-1}h^{-1/2}\right)=o_{P}\left(T^{-1}h^{-1/2}\right)

provided that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=\lfloor Th\rfloor+1}^{\lfloor T(1-h)\rfloor}\mathbf{H}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{C}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{W}}^{-1}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{R}_{T}(\tau_{t})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\left\{\frac{1}{Th}\sum_{t=\lfloor Th\rfloor+1}^{\lfloor T(1-h)\rfloor}\mathbf{H}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{C}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{W}}^{-1}(\tau_{t})K\left(\frac{\tau_{s}-\tau_{t}}{h}\right)\right\}\mathbf{W}_{s-1}\mathbf{u}_{s}=O_{P}(T^{-1/2}).

We then conclude that
T\sqrt{h}\left[\widehat{Q}_{\mathbf{C},\mathbf{H}}-(Th)^{-1}A_{Q}\right]\to_{D}N\left(0,B_{Q}\right)
. We next complete this proof by showing
T\sqrt{h}\left[\widehat{Q}_{\mathbf{C},\mathbf{H}}-\widehat{Q}_{\mathbf{C},\widehat{\mathbf{H}}}\right]=o_{P}(1)
.

Observe that

\displaystyle T\sqrt{h}\left[\widehat{Q}_{\mathbf{C},\mathbf{H}}-\widehat{Q}_{\mathbf{C},\widehat{\mathbf{H}}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T\sqrt{h}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=\lfloor Th\rfloor+1}^{\lfloor T(1-h)\rfloor}\left(\left[\mathbf{C}\widehat{\mathbf{b}}(\tau_{t})-\widehat{\mathbf{c}}\right]^{\top}\mathbf{H}(\tau_{t})\left[\mathbf{C}\widehat{\mathbf{b}}(\tau_{t})-\widehat{\mathbf{c}}\right]-\left[\mathbf{C}\widehat{\mathbf{b}}(\tau_{t})-\widehat{\mathbf{c}}\right]^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{H}}(\tau_{t})\left[\mathbf{C}\widehat{\mathbf{b}}(\tau_{t})-\widehat{\mathbf{c}}\right]\right)

\displaystyle+O_{P}(Th^{5.5}+\sqrt{h}\log T)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T\sqrt{h}\times O_{P}(\rho_{T}^{3})+O_{P}(Th^{5.5}+\sqrt{h}\log T)=o_{P}(1)

using
\sup_{\tau\in[h,1-h]}\left|\mathbf{H}(\tau)-\widehat{\mathbf{H}}(\tau)\right|=O_{P}(\rho_{T})
.
∎

Proof of Corollary
A.1.1
.

(1). Under the local alternative (9), we have
\mathbf{C}\mathbf{b}(\tau)=\mathbf{c}+d_{T}\mathbf{f}(\tau)
and thus

\displaystyle\widehat{Q}_{\mathbf{C},\mathbf{H}}-\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{R}_{T}^{\top}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{H}_{0}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{R}_{T}(\tau_{t})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d_{T}^{2}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{f}(\tau_{t})^{\top}\mathbf{H}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{f}(\tau_{t})+2d_{T}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{f}(\tau_{t})^{\top}\mathbf{H}(\tau_{t})\left(\mathbf{C}\widehat{\mathbf{b}}(\tau_{t})-\mathbf{C}\mathbf{b}(\tau_{t})\right)

\displaystyle+\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\mathbf{C}\widehat{\mathbf{b}}(\tau_{t})-\mathbf{C}\mathbf{b}(\tau_{t})\right)^{\top}\mathbf{H}(\tau_{t})\left(\mathbf{C}\widehat{\mathbf{b}}(\tau_{t})-\mathbf{C}\mathbf{b}(\tau_{t})\right)-\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{R}_{T}^{\top}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{H}_{0}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{R}_{T}(\tau_{t})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d_{T}^{2}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{f}(\tau_{t})^{\top}\mathbf{H}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{f}(\tau_{t})+I_{T,7}+I_{T,8}.

Since
\mathbf{C}\widehat{\mathbf{b}}(\tau)-\mathbf{C}\mathbf{b}(\tau)=O_{P}\left(d_{T}\rho_{T}+\sqrt{\frac{\log T}{Th}}\rho_{T}\right)+\mathbf{C}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{W}}^{-1}(\tau)\mathbf{R}_{T}(\tau)
uniformly over
\tau\in[h,1-h]
and

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{f}(\tau_{t})^{\top}\mathbf{H}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{C}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{W}}^{-1}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{R}_{T}(\tau_{t})=O_{P}(T^{-1/2}),

we have
I_{T,7}=O_{P}\left(d_{T}(d_{T}\rho_{T}+\sqrt{\frac{\log T}{Th}}\rho_{T}+T^{-1/2})\right)=o_{P}(T^{-1}h^{-1/2})
.

For
I_{T,8}
, since
\sup_{\tau\in[0,1]}\left\|\mathbf{R}_{T}(\tau)\right\|=O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log T}{Th}}\right)
, we have

I_{T,8}=O_{P}\left(d_{T}^{2}\rho_{T}^{2}+d_{T}\rho_{T}\sqrt{\frac{\log T}{Th}}\right)=o_{P}(T^{-1}h^{-1/2}).

As
T\sqrt{h}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{R}_{T}^{\top}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{H}_{0}(\tau_{t})\mathbf{R}_{T}(\tau_{t})-(Th)^{-1}A_{Q}\right)\to N(0,B_{Q})
,
we have

T\sqrt{h}\left(\widehat{Q}_{\mathbf{C},\mathbf{H}}-(Th)^{-1}A_{Q}\right)\to N(\delta_{1},B_{Q}).

In addition, similar to the proof of Theorem
2.5
, we have
T\sqrt{h}\left(\widehat{Q}_{\mathbf{C},\mathbf{H}}-\widehat{Q}_{\mathbf{C},\widehat{\mathbf{H}}}\right)=o_{P}(1)
. The proof is now completed.

(2). Part (2) follows directly from part (1).

∎

Proof of Corollary
A.1.2
.

Indeed, we can regard
\Delta\mathbf{y}_{t}^{*}
as from the data generating process (
A.1.2
) with
\bm{\alpha}^{*}(\tau)=\mathbf{0}_{d\times r_{0}}
,
\bm{\Gamma}_{j}^{*}(\tau)=\mathbf{0}_{d}
,
\bm{\omega}^{*}(\tau_{t})=\mathbf{I}_{d}
, and further we replace the cointegrated component
\bm{\beta}^{\top}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}
by
\mathbf{z}_{t}^{*}
. By using similar arguments to those used in the proof of Theorem
2.5
, we can show that

T\sqrt{h}\left(\widetilde{Q}_{\mathbf{C},\widehat{\mathbf{H}}}^{b}-\frac{1}{Th}s\widetilde{v}_{0}\right)\to_{D}N(0,4sC_{B}).

∎

A.5
Proofs of the Preliminary Lemmas

Proof of Lemma
A.1
.

(1). Define
\delta_{T}=\lfloor T(\tau-h)\rfloor
. Write

\displaystyle T^{-2}h^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(T^{-1/2}\mathbf{y}_{\delta_{T}})(T^{-1/2}\mathbf{y}_{\delta_{T}})^{\top}\cdot(Th)^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)

\displaystyle+(T^{-1/2}\mathbf{y}_{\delta_{T}})\cdot T^{-3/2}h^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\mathbf{y}_{t-1}-\mathbf{y}_{\delta_{T}})^{\top}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)

\displaystyle+T^{-3/2}h^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\mathbf{y}_{t-1}-\mathbf{y}_{\delta_{T}})K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)\cdot(T^{-1/2}\mathbf{y}_{\delta_{T}})^{\top}

\displaystyle+T^{-2}h^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\mathbf{y}_{t-1}-\mathbf{y}_{\delta_{T}})(\mathbf{y}_{t-1}-\mathbf{y}_{\delta_{T}})^{\top}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}_{T,1}+\mathbf{K}_{T,2}+\mathbf{K}_{T,3}+\mathbf{K}_{T,4}.

Consider
\mathbf{K}_{T,1}
. By part (1) and the continuous mapping theorem, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}_{T,1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(T^{-1/2}\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\sum_{t=1}^{\delta_{T}}\bm{\Psi}_{\tau_{t}}(1)\bm{\omega}(\tau_{t})\bm{\varepsilon}_{t}\right)\left(T^{-1/2}\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\sum_{t=1}^{\delta_{T}}\bm{\Psi}_{\tau_{t}}(1)\bm{\omega}(\tau_{t})\bm{\varepsilon}_{t}\right)^{\top}+O_{P}(T^{1/\delta-1/2})

\displaystyle\to_{D}

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\int_{0}^{\tau}\bm{\Psi}_{u}(1)\bm{\omega}(u)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{W}_{d}(u)\left(\int_{0}^{\tau}\bm{\Psi}_{u}(1)\bm{\omega}(u)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{W}_{d}(u)\right)^{\top}\overline{\bm{\beta}}_{\perp}\bm{\beta}_{\perp}^{\top}.

By the proof of part (1), we have
\|\mathbf{y}_{t-1}-\mathbf{y}_{\delta_{T}}\|_{\delta}=O(\sqrt{Th})
for
t\in\left[\lfloor T(\tau-h)\rfloor,\lfloor T(\tau+h)\rfloor\right]
. Hence, both
\mathbf{K}_{T,2}
and
\mathbf{K}_{T,3}
are
O_{P}(\sqrt{h})
. Similarly,
\mathbf{K}_{T,4}=O_{P}(h)
.

The proof of part (1) is now completed.

(2). Write

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{\top}(\tau)\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top}\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}(\tau)\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)\right]\bm{\Xi}_{T}(\tau)\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}=\left[\begin{matrix}\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(1)&\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(2)\\
\mathbf{K}_{T,l}^{\top}(2)&\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(3)\\
\end{matrix}\right],

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T^{2}h}\bm{\xi}_{T}^{\top}(\tau)\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top}\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}(\tau)\bm{\xi}_{T}(\tau)\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right),

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(2)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T^{2}h^{3/2}}\bm{\xi}_{T}^{\top}(\tau)\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top}\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}(\tau)\bm{\xi}_{T,\perp}(\tau)\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right),

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T^{2}h^{2}}\bm{\xi}_{T,\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top}\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}(\tau)\bm{\xi}_{T,\perp}(\tau)\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right).

Let
\delta_{T}=\lfloor T(\tau-h)\rfloor
. Consider
\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(1)
. For
\lfloor T(\tau-h)\rfloor+2\leq t\leq\lfloor T(\tau+h)\rfloor+1
, we have

\max_{t}\|1/\sqrt{T}\sum_{j=\delta_{T}+1}^{t-1}\Delta\mathbf{y}_{j}\|_{\delta}=O(\sqrt{h})

and thus

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bm{\xi}_{T}^{\top}(\tau)\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\mathbf{y}_{\delta_{T}}/\sqrt{T}(\bm{\xi}_{T}^{\top}(\tau)\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\mathbf{y}_{\delta_{T}}/\sqrt{T})^{\top}\widetilde{c}_{l}+O_{P}(\sqrt{h})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{c}_{l}\mathbf{q}_{T}^{\top}(\tau)\mathbf{q}_{T}(\tau)+O_{P}(\sqrt{h}).

Consider
\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(2)
. Since
\mathbf{q}_{T}^{\top}(\tau)\bm{\xi}_{T,\perp}(\tau)=0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(2)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bm{\xi}_{T}^{\top}(\tau)\mathbf{q}_{T}(\tau)\frac{1}{Th^{3/2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}(\mathbf{y}_{t-1}-\mathbf{y}_{\delta_{T}})^{\top}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}(\tau)\bm{\xi}_{T,\perp}(\tau)

\displaystyle+\bm{\xi}_{T}^{\top}(\tau)\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\frac{1}{Th^{3/2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}(\mathbf{y}_{t-1}-\mathbf{y}_{\delta_{T}})\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}(\mathbf{y}_{t-1}-\mathbf{y}_{\delta_{T}})^{\top}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}(\tau)\bm{\xi}_{T,\perp}(\tau)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(2,1)+\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(2,2).

Again, for
\lfloor T(\tau-h)\rfloor+2\leq t\leq\lfloor T(\tau+h)\rfloor+1
, we have

\max_{t}\|1/\sqrt{T}\sum_{j=\delta_{T}+1}^{t-1}\Delta\mathbf{y}_{j}\|_{\delta}=O(\sqrt{h})

and thus
\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(2,2)=O_{P}(\sqrt{h})
. For
\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(2,1)
, by the proof of Lemma
2.1
and
|\tau_{j}-\tau|\leq h
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2Th}}(\mathbf{y}_{t-1}-\mathbf{y}_{\delta_{T}})^{\top}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2Th}}\sum_{j=\delta_{T}+1}^{t-1}\Delta\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{j}(\tau_{j})+O_{P}(\sqrt{h/T})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2Th}}\sum_{j=\delta_{T}+1}^{t-1}\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\bm{\Psi}_{\tau_{j}}(1)\bm{\omega}(\tau_{j})\bm{\varepsilon}_{j}+O_{P}(\sqrt{h/T}+1/\sqrt{Th})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2Th}}\sum_{j=\delta_{T}+1}^{t-1}\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\bm{\Psi}_{\tau}(1)\bm{\omega}(\tau)\bm{\varepsilon}_{j}+O_{P}(\sqrt{h/T}+1/\sqrt{Th}+h),

which implies

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(2,1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bm{\xi}_{T}^{\top}(\tau)\mathbf{q}_{T}(\tau)\frac{\sqrt{2}}{Th}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2Th}}\sum_{j=\delta_{T}+1}^{t-1}\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\bm{\Psi}_{\tau}(1)\bm{\omega}(\tau)\bm{\varepsilon}_{j}\right)^{\top}

\displaystyle\times\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}(\tau)\bm{\xi}_{T,\perp}(\tau)+O_{P}(\sqrt{h}).

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bm{\xi}_{T,\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\frac{2}{Th}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2Th}}\sum_{j=\delta_{T}+1}^{t-1}\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\bm{\Psi}_{\tau}(1)\bm{\omega}(\tau)\bm{\varepsilon}_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2Th}}\sum_{j=\delta_{T}+1}^{t-1}\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\bm{\Psi}_{\tau}(1)\bm{\omega}(\tau)\bm{\varepsilon}_{j}\right)^{\top}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}(\tau)\bm{\xi}_{T,\perp}(\tau)+O_{P}(h).

Furthermore, by the usual functional central limit theory (e.g., Theorem 4.1 in
Hall & Heyde 1980
), we have

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{\delta_{T}}\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\bm{\Psi}_{\tau_{t}}(1)\bm{\omega}(\tau_{t})\bm{\varepsilon}_{t},\frac{1}{\sqrt{2Th}}\sum_{t=\delta_{T}+1}^{\delta_{T}(u)}\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\bm{\Psi}_{\tau}(1)\bm{\omega}(\tau)\bm{\varepsilon}_{t}\right)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{W}_{d}(\tau,\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{y}}(\tau)),\mathbf{W}_{d}^{*}(u,\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\alpha}}(\tau))\right),

where
\delta_{T}(u)=\delta_{T}+\lfloor 2uTh\rfloor+1
and
\mathbf{W}_{d}^{*}(\cdot,\cdot)
is independent of
\mathbf{W}_{d}(\cdot,\cdot)
. Note that the Lindeberg condition can be verified as
\|\bm{\Psi}_{\tau_{t}}(1)\bm{\omega}(\tau_{t})\bm{\varepsilon}_{t}\|_{\delta}<\infty
for some
\delta>2
, and the convergence of conditional variance can be verified as
E(\bm{\varepsilon}_{t}\bm{\varepsilon}_{t}^{\top}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1})=\mathbf{I}_{d}
a.s. Then part (2) follows directly from the continuous mapping theorem.

(3). Write

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\mathbf{D}_{T}^{-1}\bm{\Xi}_{T}^{\top}(\tau)\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top}\bm{\beta}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\begin{matrix}\frac{1}{T^{3/2}h}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\xi}_{T}^{\top}(\tau)\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\mathbf{z}_{t-1}^{\top}\bm{\beta}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)\\
\frac{1}{T^{3/2}h^{3/2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\xi}_{T,\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\mathbf{z}_{t-1}^{\top}\bm{\beta}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)\\
\end{matrix}\right]=\left[\begin{matrix}\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(4)\\
\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(5)\\
\end{matrix}\right].

Consider
\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(4)
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T^{3/2}h}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\xi}_{T}^{\top}(\tau)\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\mathbf{z}_{t-1}^{\top}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Th}\bm{\xi}_{T}^{\top}(\tau)\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\mathbf{y}_{\delta_{T}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau)(\mathbf{y}_{t-1}-\mathbf{y}_{\delta_{T}})\right)\mathbf{z}_{t-1}^{\top}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathbf{q}_{T}^{\top}(\tau)\mathbf{q}_{T}(\tau)}\frac{1}{Th}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t-1}^{\top}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\sqrt{h}}{Th}\bm{\xi}_{T}^{\top}(\tau)\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau)(\mathbf{y}_{t-1}-\mathbf{y}_{\delta_{T}})\mathbf{z}_{t-1}^{\top}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(4,1)+\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(4,2).

Similar to the proof of Lemma
2.1
, by
\max_{t}\|\mathbf{z}_{t}-\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t}(\tau_{t})\|_{\delta}=O(1/T)
and using BN decomposition, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Th}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{z}_{t-1}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Th}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t-1}(\tau_{t-1})\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)+O_{P}(1/T)=O_{P}(1/\sqrt{Th})

and thus
\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(4,1)=O_{P}(1/\sqrt{Th})
. For
\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(4,2)
, by Lemma
2.1
(1), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Th}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\sum_{j=\delta_{T}+1}^{t-1}\Delta\mathbf{y}_{j}\mathbf{z}_{t-1}^{\top}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Th}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\sum_{j=\delta_{T}+1}^{t-1}\Delta\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{j}(\tau_{j})\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t-1}^{\top}(\tau_{t-1})\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)+O_{P}(\sqrt{h/T}).

Note that
\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\sum_{j=\delta_{T}+1}^{t-1}\Delta\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{j}(\tau_{j})=\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\sum_{j=\delta_{T}+1}^{t-1}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{j}(\tau_{j})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\sum_{j=\delta_{T}+1}^{t-1}\Delta\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{j}(\tau_{j})
. Similarly, by Lemma
2.1
, we have

\displaystyle\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\sum_{j=\delta_{T}+1}^{t-1}\Delta\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{j}(\tau_{j})\|_{\delta/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\|\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{\delta_{T}}(\tau_{\delta_{T}+1})\|_{\delta/2}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\|\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t-1}(\tau_{t-1})\|_{\delta/2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\|\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}}\sum_{j=\delta_{T}+1}^{t-1}(\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{j}(\tau_{j})-\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{j}(\tau_{j+1}))\|_{\delta/2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O(1/\sqrt{Th}).

In addition, since
\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t}(\tau)=\bm{\Psi}_{\tau}(1)\bm{\omega}(\tau)\bm{\varepsilon}_{t}+\bm{\Psi}_{\tau}^{*}(L)\bm{\omega}(\tau)\bm{\varepsilon}_{t-1}-\bm{\Psi}_{\tau}^{*}(L)\bm{\omega}(\tau)\bm{\varepsilon}_{t}
,
we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Th}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\sum_{j=\delta_{T}+1}^{t-1}\Delta\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{j}(\tau_{j})\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t-1}^{\top}(\tau_{t-1})\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Th}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\sum_{j=\delta_{T}+1}^{t-1}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{j}(\tau_{j})\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t-1}^{\top}(\tau_{t-1})\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)+O_{P}(1/\sqrt{Th})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\frac{1}{Th}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\sum_{j=\delta_{T}+1}^{t-1}\bm{\Psi}_{\tau_{j}}(1)\bm{\omega}(\tau_{j})\bm{\varepsilon}_{j}(\bm{\Psi}_{\tau_{t-1}}(1)\bm{\omega}(\tau_{t-1})\bm{\varepsilon}_{t-1})^{\top}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\frac{1}{Th}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\sum_{j=\delta_{T}+1}^{t-1}\bm{\Psi}_{\tau_{j}}(1)\bm{\omega}(\tau_{j})\bm{\varepsilon}_{j}(\bm{\Psi}_{\tau_{t-1}}^{*}(L)\bm{\omega}(\tau_{t-1})\bm{\varepsilon}_{t-2}-\bm{\Psi}_{\tau_{t-1}}^{*}(L)\bm{\omega}(\tau_{t-1})\bm{\varepsilon}_{t-1})^{\top}

\displaystyle\times\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\frac{1}{Th}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\bm{\Psi}_{\tau_{\delta_{T}+1}}^{*}(L)\bm{\omega}(\tau_{\delta_{T}+1})\bm{\varepsilon}_{\delta_{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t-1}^{\top}(\tau_{t-1})\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\frac{1}{Th}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\bm{\Psi}_{\tau_{t-1}}^{*}(L)\bm{\omega}(\tau_{t-1})\bm{\varepsilon}_{t-1}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t-1}^{\top}(\tau_{t-1})\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)

\displaystyle+\mathbf{P}_{\bm{\beta}_{\perp}}\frac{1}{Th}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{\sqrt{Th}}\sum_{j=\delta_{T}+1}^{t-2}\left(\bm{\Psi}_{\tau_{j+1}}^{*}(L)\bm{\omega}(\tau_{j+1})-\bm{\Psi}_{\tau_{j}}^{*}(L)\bm{\omega}(\tau_{j})\right)\bm{\varepsilon}_{j}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{t-1}^{\top}(\tau_{t-1})\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(4,21)+\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(4,22)+O_{P}(1/\sqrt{Th}).

Since
\sum_{j=\delta_{T}+1}^{t-2}\bm{\varepsilon}_{j}\bm{\varepsilon}_{t-1}^{\top}
is a sequence of martingale differences, by using Burkholder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=\delta_{T}+1}^{t-2}\bm{\varepsilon}_{j}\bm{\varepsilon}_{t-1}^{\top}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)\right\|_{\delta/2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(1)\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\|\sum_{j=\delta_{T}+1}^{t-2}\bm{\varepsilon}_{j}\bm{\varepsilon}_{t-1}^{\top}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)\right\|_{\delta/2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O(1)\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\|\sum_{j=\delta_{T}+1}^{t-2}\bm{\varepsilon}_{j}\right\|_{\delta}^{2}\left\|\bm{\varepsilon}_{t-1}\right\|_{\delta}^{2}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{2l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{2}=O(T^{2}h^{2}).

Therefore,
\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(4,21)=O_{P}(1/\sqrt{Th})
. For
\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(4,22)
, let
\mathbf{p}_{t}=\sum_{j=\delta_{T}+1}^{t}\bm{\Psi}_{\tau_{j}}(1)\bm{\omega}(\tau_{j})\bm{\varepsilon}_{j}
,
\mathbf{w}_{t}=\bm{\Psi}_{\tau}^{*}(L)\bm{\omega}(\tau)\bm{\varepsilon}_{t}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{p}_{t-1}(\mathbf{w}_{t-2}-\mathbf{w}_{t-1})^{\top}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{p}_{t-1}\mathbf{w}_{t-2}^{\top}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)-\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{p}_{t-1}\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{\top}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{p}_{t-2}\mathbf{w}_{t-2}^{\top}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)-\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{p}_{t-1}\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{\top}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)+O_{P}(\sqrt{Th})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{p}_{t-2}\mathbf{w}_{t-2}^{\top}\left(\frac{\tau_{t-1}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t-1}-\tau}{h}\right)-\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbf{p}_{t-1}\mathbf{w}_{t-1}^{\top}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)+O_{P}(\sqrt{Th})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{p}_{-1}\mathbf{w}_{-1}^{\top}\left(\frac{\tau_{0}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{0}-\tau}{h}\right)-\mathbf{p}_{T-1}\mathbf{w}_{T-1}^{\top}\left(\frac{\tau_{T}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{T}-\tau}{h}\right)+O_{P}(\sqrt{Th})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}(\sqrt{Th}).

Hence,
\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(4,22)=O_{P}(1/(Th))
. Therefore,
\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(4,2)=O_{P}(1/\sqrt{T})
and thus
\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(4)=O_{P}(1/\sqrt{Th})
.

Consider
\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(5)
. As
\bm{\xi}_{T,\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\mathbf{y}_{\delta_{T}}=0
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T^{3/2}h^{3/2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\xi}_{T,\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\mathbf{z}_{t-1}^{\top}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Th^{3/2}}\bm{\xi}_{T,\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=\delta_{T}+1}^{t-1}\Delta\mathbf{y}_{j}\mathbf{z}_{t-1}^{\top}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)

Similar to the proof of
\mathbf{K}_{T,l}(4,2)
, we have

\frac{1}{Th^{3/2}}\bm{\xi}_{T,\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\bm{\alpha}_{\perp}^{\top}(\tau)\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{j=\delta_{T}+1}^{t-1}\Delta\mathbf{y}_{j}\mathbf{z}_{t-1}^{\top}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)=O_{P}(1/\sqrt{Th}).

The proof of part (3) is now completed.

(4). Since
\bm{\beta}^{\top}\mathbf{y}_{t}=\bm{\beta}^{\top}\mathbf{z}_{t}
, by Lemma
2.1
and using BN decomposition developed in the proof of Lemma
2.1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Th}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bm{\beta}^{\top}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top}\bm{\beta}\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)^{l}K\left(\frac{\tau_{t}-\tau}{h}\right)

\displaystyle=
